is Bochner integrable, then

(A.1)

\left\|\int_{a}^{b}u(t)\,{\mathrm{d}}t\right\|\leq\int_{a}^{b}\|u(t)\|\,{\mathrm{d}}t,\qquad\text{for any }[a,b]\subseteq[0,T],

(A.2)

\lim_{h\to 0}\frac{1}{h}\int_{t}^{t+h}\|u(s)-u(t)\|\,{\mathrm{d}}s=0,\qquad\text{for ${\mathcal{L}}_{T}$-a.e. }t\in[0,T],

and the above limit exists in every point of continuity of
u
.
Moreover, for every continuous linear operator
A:E\to\tilde{E}
, with
\tilde{E}
a Banach space it holds that

(A.3)

A\left(\int_{0}^{T}u(t)\,{\mathrm{d}}t\right)=\int_{0}^{T}A(u(t))\,{\mathrm{d}}t.

For the definition of Bochner integral, properties and related proofs, see for instance
[
26
]
.

We say that
u\in W^{1,p}(0,T;E)
if
u\in L^{p}(0,T;E)
and there exists
g\in L^{p}(0,T;E)
such that

\int_{0}^{T}\varphi^{\prime}(t)u(t)\,{\mathrm{d}}t=-\int_{0}^{T}\varphi(t)g(t)\,{\mathrm{d}}t,\quad\forall\,\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{c}((0,T);\mathbb{R}).

We recall a classical result (see e.g.
[
4
, Theorem 1.17]
)

Proposition A.1
.

u\in W^{1,p}(0,T;E)
if and only if there exists
\tilde{u}\in\mathrm{AC}^{p}([0,T];E)
such that
\tilde{u}(t)=u(t)
and
\tilde{u}
is differentiable for
{\mathcal{L}}_{T}
-a.e.
t\in[0,T]
.

Let now
(\Omega,\mathfrak{B},\mathbb{P})
be a probability space with
\Omega
standard Borel.
Fix
T>0
, let
{\mathrm{Leb}}_{[0,T]}
be the
\sigma
-algebra of Lebesgue measurable sets on
[0,T]
and
{\mathcal{L}}_{T}
the normalized Lebesgue measure restricted to
[0,T]
. Consider
(\Omega_{T},\mathfrak{B}_{T},\mathfrak{m})
the product space with

\Omega_{T}:=[0,T]\times\Omega
, endowed with the product

\sigma
-algebra
\mathfrak{B}_{T}={\mathrm{Leb}}_{[0,T]}\otimes\mathfrak{B}
and probability measure
\mathfrak{m}:={\mathcal{L}}_{T}\otimes\mathbb{P}\in\mathscr{P}(\Omega_{T})
.

Lemma A.2
.

Let
p\geq 1
, and
g\in L^{p}_{\mathfrak{m}}(\Omega_{T};\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\hat{g}
a Borel representative of
g
.
Let
\tilde{g}
the map defined by
\tilde{g}(t)(\omega)=\hat{g}(t,\omega)
for every
(t,\omega)\in\Omega_{T}
.
Then
\tilde{g}\in L^{1}(0,T;L^{p}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d}))
and,
denoting by
G:=\int_{0}^{T}\tilde{g}(t)\,{\mathrm{d}}t
,
it holds

(A.4)

G(\omega)=\int_{0}^{T}\hat{g}(t,\omega)\,{\mathrm{d}}t,\quad\text{ for }\mathbb{P}\text{-a.e. }\omega\in\Omega.

The proof of the Lemma follows by Fubini’s theorem and the definition of Bochner integral (notice that (
A.4
) holds for simple functions).

Proposition A.3
.

Let
y\in L^{p}_{\mathfrak{m}}(\Omega_{T};\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
The following are equivalent:

(1)

There exists
g\in L^{p}_{\mathfrak{m}}(\Omega_{T};\mathbb{R}^{d})

such that

\int_{\Omega_{T}}\eta^{\prime}(t)\phi(\omega)\,y(t,\omega)\,{\mathrm{d}}\mathfrak{m}(t,\omega)=-\int_{\Omega_{T}}\eta(t)\phi(\omega)\,g(t,\omega)\,{\mathrm{d}}\mathfrak{m}(t,\omega),

for every
\eta\in C^{1}_{c}((0,T);\mathbb{R})
and
\phi\in\mathrm{M}(\Omega;\mathbb{R})
bounded;

(2)

y\in W^{1,p}(0,T;L^{p}_{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d}))
;

(3)

there exists a Borel representative
\tilde{y}
of
y

such that
\tilde{y}\in\mathrm{AC}^{p}([0,T];L^{p}_{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d}))
and differentiable for
{\mathcal{L}}_{T}
-a.e.
t\in[0,T]
(differentiability is redundant for
p>1
);

(4)

y\in L^{p}_{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega;W^{1,p}(0,T;\mathbb{R}^{d}))
;

(5)

there exists a Borel representative
\bar{y}
of
y
such that
\bar{y}\in L^{p}_{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega;\mathrm{AC}^{p}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{d}))
.

Moreover,

(i)

If
(3)
holds, there exists a Borel function
\tilde{g}\in L^{p}_{\mathfrak{m}}(\Omega_{T};\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that
\tilde{g}(t,\cdot)=\tilde{y}^{\prime}(t,\cdot)
in
L^{p}_{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})
, for
{\mathcal{L}}_{T}
-a.e.
t\in[0,T]
.
Hence, for every
t\in[0,T]

(A.5)

\tilde{y}(t,\cdot)=\tilde{y}(0,\cdot)+\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{g}(s,\cdot)\,{\mathrm{d}}s,\quad\text{ in }L^{p}_{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega).

(ii)

If
(5)
holds, there exists a Borel function
\bar{g}\in L^{p}_{\mathfrak{m}}(\Omega_{T};\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that for every
\omega\in\Omega
,
\bar{g}(t,\omega)=(\bar{y}(\cdot,\omega))^{\prime}(t)
, for
{\mathcal{L}}_{T}
-a.e.
t\in[0,T]
.
Hence, for every
\omega\in\Omega
it holds

(A.6)

\bar{y}(t,\omega)=\bar{y}(0,\omega)+\int_{0}^{t}\bar{g}(s,\omega){\mathrm{d}}s,\quad\text{for every }t\in[0,T].

(iii)

If one of the five conditions above is satisfied, then

g=\tilde{g}=\bar{g}
,
\mathfrak{m}
-a.e. in
\Omega_{T}
.

Proof.

(5)\Rightarrow(4)
and
(3)\Leftrightarrow(2)
follow from Proposition
A.1
.

(4)\Rightarrow(2)
follows from the definition and Fubini’s theorem.

(2)\Rightarrow(1)
is a consequence of property (
A.3
).
Finally, the proof of
(1)\Rightarrow(5)
is contained in a more general form in
[
45
, Lemma 4.3]
.

Items
(i)
-
(ii)
are a consequence of the definition of
\mathrm{AC}^{p}([0,T];L^{p}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d}))
and
\mathrm{AC}^{p}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{d})
, respectively.
To show
(iii)
, denote with
y_{0}(\omega)
the trace of
y
in
t=0
, which is well defined thanks to
(1)
.
Then,
y_{0}(\omega)=\tilde{y}(0,\omega)=\bar{y}(0,\omega)
for
\mathbb{P}
-a.e.
\omega\in\Omega
so that comparing (
A.5
) and (
A.6
) it holds
\tilde{g}=\bar{g}

\mathfrak{m}
-a.e. in
\Omega_{T}
, thanks to (
A.4
).
∎

A.1.
Cauchy problem in Banach spaces

We are interested in a Cauchy problem of this form

(A.7)

\begin{cases}\dot{z}_{t}=F(t,z_{t}),\qquad\text{for ${\mathcal{L}}_{T}$-a.e. }t\in[0,T]\\
z_{t=0}=z_{0},\end{cases}

where a Carathéodory function
F:[0,T]\times E\to E
and
z_{0}\in E
are given.
For
z:[0,T]\to E
we frequently use the notation
z_{t}:=z(t)
.

In the following, we present some classical results concerning the Cauchy problem (
A.7
) and we provide a sketch of
their proofs.

Proposition A.4
.

Let
F:[0,T]\times E\to E
be a Carathéodory function such that

(A.8)

\int_{0}^{T}\|F(t,z_{t})\|\,{\mathrm{d}}t<+\infty,\qquad\forall z\in C([0,T];E).

The following assertions are equivalent:

•

z\in C([0,T];E)
satisfies

(A.9)

z_{t}=z_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}F(s,z_{s})\,{\mathrm{d}}s,\qquad\forall\,t\in[0,T].

•

z\in\mathrm{AC}^{1}([0,T];E)
, it is differentiable for
{\mathcal{L}}_{T}
-a.e.
t\in[0,T]
and

\begin{cases}\dot{z}_{t}=F(t,z_{t}),\qquad\text{for ${\mathcal{L}}_{T}$-a.e. }t\in[0,T]\\
z_{t=0}=z_{0}.\end{cases}

Since
F
is a Carathéodory function and the curve
t\mapsto z_{t}
then the map
t\mapsto F(t,z_{t})
is (strongly) measurable as a map with values in
L^{p}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
Notice also that
if
E
satisfies the Radon-Nikodym property (for instance when
E
is reflexive) then a.e. differentiability of
z
in the second item is redundant.

Theorem A.5
.

Let
F:[0,T]\times E\to E
be a Carathéodory function such that

(A.10)

\|F(t,z^{1})-F(t,z^{2})\|\leq\tilde{L}\|z^{1}-z^{2}\|,\qquad\forall\,(t,z^{1}),(t,z^{2})\in[0,T]\times E,

for some
\tilde{L}>0
, and there exists
C_{0}\geq 0
such that

(A.11)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|F(t,0)\|\leq C_{0}<+\infty.

Then, for any
z_{0}\in E
there exists a unique
z\in\mathrm{AC}^{\infty}([0,T];E)
and differentiable for
{\mathcal{L}}_{T}
-a.e.
t\in[0,T]

such that

(A.12)

\begin{cases}\dot{z}_{t}=F(t,z_{t}),\qquad\text{for ${\mathcal{L}}_{T}$-a.e. }t\in[0,T]\\
z(0)=z_{0}.\end{cases}

Moreover, the following estimates hold:

(A.13)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|z_{t}\|\leq e^{\tilde{L}T}\left(\|z_{0}\|+C_{0}T\right),

(A.14)

\|z_{t}-z_{s}\|\leq|t-s|\,\big(C_{0}+\tilde{L}e^{\tilde{L}T}\left(\|z_{0}\|+C_{0}T\right)\big)\qquad\forall\,s,t\in[0,T].

Proof.

We provide only a sketch of the proof.

Assumptions (
A.10
) and (
A.11
) yield the following growth property

(A.15)

\|F(t,z)\|\leq C_{0}+\tilde{L}\|z\|,\qquad\forall\,(t,z)\in[0,T]\times E.

We define the Banach space
(\mathscr{S},\|\cdot\|_{\mathscr{S}})
as follows

\mathscr{S}:=\left\{z\in C([0,T];E)\,:\,\|z\|_{\mathscr{S}}:=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-\tilde{L}t}\|z_{t}\|<+\infty\right\},

and the operator

S:\mathscr{S}\to\mathscr{S}
by

S(y)_{t}=z_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}F(s,y_{s})\,{\mathrm{d}}s.

By (
A.10
) and (
A.15
), using (
A.1
),
it is classical to prove that
S
is well defined and it is a contraction.
Then, by Banach fixed point Theorem
we get the existence and uniqueness of
z\in\mathscr{S}
such that (
A.9
) holds.
The estimates (
A.13
) and (
A.14
) follow from (
A.9
), (
A.15
), and Gronwall inequality.
Finally,
z
belongs to
\mathrm{AC}^{\infty}([0,T];E)
thanks to (
A.14
).
∎

Proposition A.6
.

Let
F,F^{n}:[0,T]\times E\to E
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, be Carathéodory functions satisfying
(
A.10
),(
A.11
) with the same constant
\tilde{L}
and
C_{0}
.
Let
z_{0},z^{n}_{0}\in E
,
z\in\mathrm{AC}^{\infty}([0,T];E)
the solution of (
A.12
) and
z^{n}\in\mathrm{AC}^{\infty}([0,T];E)
the solutions of

\begin{cases}\dot{z}^{n}_{t}=F^{n}(t,z^{n}_{t}),\qquad\text{for ${\mathcal{L}}_{T}$-a.e. }t\in[0,T]\\
z^{n}(0)=z^{n}_{0}.\end{cases}

Then

(A.16)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|z^{n}_{t}-z_{t}\|\leq e^{\tilde{L}T}\left(\|z^{n}_{0}-z_{0}\|+\int_{0}^{T}\|F^{n}(t,z_{t})-F(t,z_{t})\|\,{\mathrm{d}}t\right).

In particular, if
\lim_{n\to+\infty}\|z_{0}^{n}-z_{0}\|=0
and

(A.17)

\lim_{n\to+\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\|F^{n}(t,z_{t})-F(t,z_{t})\|\,{\mathrm{d}}t=0,

then

\lim_{n\to+\infty}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|z^{n}_{t}-z_{t}\|=0.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\|z^{n}_{t}-z_{t}\|

\displaystyle=\left\|z^{n}_{0}-z_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}(F^{n}(s,z^{n}_{s})-F(s,z_{s}))\,{\mathrm{d}}s\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\|z^{n}_{0}-z_{0}\|+\int_{0}^{t}\|F^{n}(s,z^{n}_{s})-F(s,z_{s}))\|\,{\mathrm{d}}s.

Using (
A.10
) it holds

\displaystyle\|F^{n}(s,z^{n}_{s})-F(s,z_{s}))\|

\displaystyle\leq\|F^{n}(s,z^{n}_{s})-F^{n}(s,z_{s}))\|+\|F^{n}(s,z_{s})-F(s,z_{s}))\|

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{L}\|z^{n}_{s}-z_{s}\|+\|F^{n}(s,z_{s})-F(s,z_{s}))\|.

The last two inequalities yield

\|z^{n}_{t}-z_{t}\|\leq\|z^{n}_{0}-z_{0}\|+\int_{0}^{T}\|F^{n}(s,z_{s})-F(s,z_{s}))\|\,{\mathrm{d}}s+\tilde{L}\int_{0}^{t}\|z^{n}_{s}-z_{s}\|\,{\mathrm{d}}s.

By Gronwall lemma we obtain (
A.16
).
∎

Proposition A.7
.

Let
F:[0,T]\times L^{p}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})\to L^{p}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})
be a Carathéodory function satisfying (
A.11
)
Let
Y_{0}\in L^{p}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
Then the following assertions are equivalent:

(1)

Y\in C([0,T];L^{p}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d}))
and

(A.18)

Y_{t}=Y_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}F(s,Y_{s})\,{\mathrm{d}}s,\qquad\forall\,t\in[0,T];

(2)

Y\in\mathrm{AC}^{p}([0,T];L^{p}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d}))
(if
p=1
it is also differentiable for a.e.
t\in[0,T]
) and

(A.19)

\begin{cases}\dot{Y}_{t}=F(t,Y_{t}),\qquad\text{for ${\mathcal{L}}_{T}$-a.e. }t\in[0,T]\\
Y_{t=0}=Y_{0};\end{cases}

(3)

Y\in L^{p}(\Omega;\mathrm{AC}^{p}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{d})))
and

(A.20)

Y_{t}(\omega)=Y_{0}(\omega)+\int_{0}^{t}F(s,Y_{s})(\omega)\,{\mathrm{d}}s,\qquad\forall\,t\in[0,T],\quad\text{for $\mathbb{P}$-a.e. }\omega\in\Omega,

where
Y_{t}:\Omega\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
is defined by
Y_{t}(\omega):=Y(t,\omega)
for
\mathbb{P}
-a.e.
\omega\in\Omega
.

(4)

Y\in L^{p}(\Omega;\mathrm{AC}^{p}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{d})))
and
for
\mathbb{P}
-a.e.
\omega\in\Omega
it holds

(A.21)

\begin{cases}\dot{Y}_{t}(\omega)=F(t,Y_{t})(\omega),\qquad\text{for ${\mathcal{L}}_{T}$-a.e. }t\in[0,T]\\
Y_{t=0}(\omega)=Y_{0}(\omega),\end{cases}

where
Y_{t}:\Omega\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
is defined by
Y_{t}(\omega):=Y(t,\omega)
for
\mathbb{P}
-a.e.
\omega\in\Omega
.

Proof.

The assertions (1)-(2) and (3)-(4) are equivalent by Proposition
A.4
.
The equivalence (1)-(3) is a consequence of the equivalences (3)-(5) in Proposition
A.3
and items (i)-(ii)-(iii).
∎

Appendix B
A convergence result for solutions of Cauchy problems

We state and prove the following well known result, for sake of completeness.

Lemma B.1
.

Let
U
be a Polish space.
Let
u^{n}:[0,T]\to U
be a sequence of
{\mathcal{L}}_{T}
-measurable functions such that

(i_{[0,T]},u^{n})_{\sharp}{\mathcal{L}}_{T}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{Y}}\nu_{t}\otimes{\mathcal{L}}_{T}\in\mathscr{P}([0,T]\times U)
.

Let
g:[0,T]\times(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times U)\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
a Carathéodory function
such that

(B.1)

|g(t,x_{1},u)-g(t,x_{2},u)|\leq L|x_{1}-x_{2}|,\qquad\forall\,(t,x_{1},u),(t,x_{2},u)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times U,

for some
L>0
, and

(B.2)

C_{0}:=\sup_{(t,u)\in[0,T]\times U}|g(t,0,u)|<+\infty.

Given
X_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we denote by
X^{n}\in\mathrm{AC}^{1}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{d})
the unique solution of the Cauchy problem

(B.3)

\begin{cases}\dot{X}^{n}_{t}=g(t,X^{n}_{t},u^{n}_{t}),&\textrm{for a.e. }t\in(0,T)\\
X^{n}(0)=X_{0},\end{cases}

and by
X\in\mathrm{AC}^{1}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{d})
the unique solution of the Cauchy problem

(B.4)

\begin{cases}\dot{X}_{t}=\displaystyle\int_{U}g(t,X_{t},u)\,{\mathrm{d}}\nu_{t}(u),&\textrm{for a.e. }t\in(0,T)\\
X(0)=X_{0}.\end{cases}

Then

(B.5)

\lim_{n\to+\infty}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|X^{n}_{t}-X_{t}|=0.

Proof.

Observe that existence and uniqueness of solutions of Cauchy problems (
B.3
) and (
B.4
) is consequence of
the fact that
G^{n},G:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
, defined by
G^{n}(t,x):=g(t,x,u^{n}_{t})
and
G(t,x):=\displaystyle\int_{U}g(t,x,u)\,{\mathrm{d}}\nu_{t}(u)
,
are Charathéodory and
L
-Lipschitz continuous w.r.t.
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Moreover, by (
B.1
) and (
B.2
) it holds

(B.6)

|g(t,x,u)|\leq C_{0}+L|x|,\qquad\forall\,(t,x,u)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times U.

We define
Y^{n}\in C([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{d})
by

(B.7)

Y^{n}_{t}:=X_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}g(s,X_{s},u^{n}_{s})\,{\mathrm{d}}s.

From the convergence
(i_{[0,T]},u^{n})_{\sharp}{\mathcal{L}}_{T}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{Y}}\nu\in\mathscr{P}([0,T]\times U)
it follows that

(B.8)

\lim_{n\to+\infty}Y^{n}_{t}=X_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{U}g(s,X_{s},u)\,{\mathrm{d}}\nu_{s}(u)\,{\mathrm{d}}s=X_{t},\qquad\forall\,t\in[0,T].

Denoting by
C:=\sup_{s\in[0,T]}|X_{s}|
, from (
B.2
) and (
B.1
) it is simple to prove that

(B.9)

|Y^{n}_{t}|\leq(|X_{0}|+(C_{0}+LC)T),\qquad\forall t\in[0,T]

and

(B.10)

|Y^{n}_{t}-Y^{n}_{s}|\leq|t-s|(C_{0}+LC)T,\qquad\forall t,s\in[0,T].

By (
B.9
) and (
B.10
), Ascoli-Arzelà theorem and (
B.8
) imply that

(B.11)

\lim_{n\to+\infty}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|Y^{n}_{t}-X_{t}|=0.

Since

\begin{split}|X^{n}_{t}-X_{t}|&\leq|X^{n}_{t}-Y^{n}_{t}|+|Y^{n}_{t}-X_{t}|\leq\int_{0}^{t}\left|g(s,X^{n}_{s},u^{n}_{s})-g(s,X_{s},u^{n}_{s})\right|\,{\mathrm{d}}s+|Y^{n}_{t}-X_{t}|\\
&\leq L\int_{0}^{t}|X^{n}_{s}-X_{s}|\,{\mathrm{d}}s+\sup_{s\in[0,T]}|Y^{n}_{s}-X_{s}|,\end{split}

by Gronwall inequality we have that

(B.12)

|X^{n}_{t}-X_{t}|\leq e^{Lt}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}|Y^{n}_{s}-X_{s}|.

The convergence (
B.5
) follows from (
B.12
) and (
B.11
).
∎

Appendix C
An empirical Superposition Principle

In this appendix, we give a refined version of the Superposition Principle (see Theorem
2.5
for the classical result) in the case of trajectories of the form
\mu_{t}\in\mathscr{P}^{N}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
for any
t\in[0,T]
, where
\mathscr{P}^{N}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is the space of empirical probability measures

\mathscr{P}^{N}(\mathbb{R}^{d}):=\left\{\mu=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\delta_{x_{i}}\;\text{ for some }x_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\right\}.

The novelty consists in proving that if
\mu_{t}\in\mathscr{P}^{N}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
for every
t\in[0,T]
, then there exists representative
\eta\in\mathscr{P}^{N}(\Gamma_{T})
.
This result has been used to prove Proposition
9.8
and Corollary
9.3
.

Theorem C.1
.

Let
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
\mu\in\mathrm{AC}([0,T];\mathscr{P}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
such that
\mu_{t}\in\mathscr{P}^{N}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
for every
t\in[0,T]
.

(1)

There exists a unique (up to
{\mathcal{L}}_{T}\otimes\mu_{t}
-negligible sets)
Borel vector field
v:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
satisfying

(C.1)

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|v_{t}(x)|\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{t}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}t<+\infty,

such that
\mu
is a distributional solution of the continuity equation

(C.2)

\partial_{t}\mu_{t}+\mathrm{div}(v_{t}\mu_{t})=0,\quad\text{in }[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}.

(2)

There exists
\eta\in\mathscr{P}^{N}(\Gamma_{T})
of the form

(C.3)

\eta=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\delta_{\gamma_{i}},

such that
(e_{t})_{\sharp}\eta=\mu_{t}
for every
t\in[0,T]
and
for any
i=1,\dots,N
,
\gamma_{i}\in\mathrm{AC}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{d})

solves the differential equation

(C.4)

\dot{\gamma}_{i}(t)=v_{t}(\gamma_{i}(t))\quad\text{for }{\mathcal{L}}_{T}\text{-a.e. }t\in[0,T].

Proof.

Let us recall that the metric derivative
|\mu^{\prime}|
of the absolutely continuous curve
\mu
, given by

|\mu^{\prime}|(t):=\lim_{s\to t}\frac{W_{1}(\mu_{t},\mu_{s})}{|t-s|},\quad\text{for a.e. }t\in[0,T],

belongs to
L^{1}(0,T)
and satisfies

(C.5)

W_{1}(\mu_{t_{1}},\mu_{t_{2}})\leq\displaystyle\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}|\mu^{\prime}|(t)\,{\mathrm{d}}t,\quad\text{ for any }0\leq t_{1}\leq t_{2}\leq T.

in particular
there exists
\psi:[0,+\infty)\to[0,+\infty)
increasing, convex and superlinear at
+\infty
such that

(C.6)

\int_{0}^{T}\psi\big(|\mu^{\prime}|(t)\big)\,{\mathrm{d}}t<+\infty.

First of all we prove the existence of
\eta\in\mathscr{P}(\Gamma_{T})
such that
(e_{t})_{\sharp}\eta=\mu_{t}
for all
t\in[0,T]
and
\eta
is of the form (
C.3
) for some
\gamma_{i}\in\Gamma_{T}
,
i=1,\dots,N
.

Let
M\in\mathbb{N}
and consider the diadic discretization of the interval
[0,T]
, with time step
\tau_{M}=T\,2^{-M}
.
Since
\mu_{t}\in\mathscr{P}^{N}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
for every
t\in[0,T]
, there exists
x_{i}(t)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
i=1,\ldots,N
, such that

\mu_{t}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\delta_{x_{i}(t)},\qquad\forall\,t\in[0,T].

For
n=0,\dots,2^{M}
and
i=1,\dots,N
, we set
x^{n}_{M,i}:=x_{i}(n\tau_{M})
, and
\mu^{n}_{M}:=\mu_{n\tau_{M}}
.
For
n=1,\dots,2^{M}
, let
\varrho^{n-1,n}_{M}\in\Gamma_{o}(\mu^{n-1}_{M},\mu^{n}_{M})
be an optimal plan for the
1
-Wasserstein distance.
Since
\mu^{n-1}_{M}
and
\mu^{n}_{M}
belong to
\mathscr{P}^{N}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, then
\varrho^{n-1,n}_{M}
is of the form

(C.7)

\varrho_{M}^{n-1,n}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\delta_{x^{n-1}_{M,i}}\otimes\delta_{x^{n}_{M,\sigma^{n}_{M}(i)}},

for some permutation
\sigma^{n}_{M}
of
\{1,\dots,N\}
.
Let us define
\sigma_{M}^{0,n}:=\sigma_{M}^{n}\circ\sigma_{M}^{n-1}\circ\dots\circ\sigma_{M}^{1}
and
\sigma_{M}^{0,0}(i)=i
for
i=1,\ldots,N
.

For
i=1,\dots,N
we define the curves
\gamma_{M,i}\in\Gamma_{T}
by linear time interpolation as

\gamma_{M,i}(t):=\frac{n\tau_{M}-t}{\tau_{M}}\,x^{n-1}_{M,\sigma_{M}^{0,n-1}(i)}+\frac{t-(n-1)\tau_{M}}{\tau_{M}}\,x^{n}_{M,\sigma_{M}^{0,n}(i)},\quad\text{for }t\in[(n-1)\tau_{M},n\tau_{M}],

n=1,\dots,2^{M}
.

We claim that, for any
k=1,\ldots,N
, the sequence
\{\gamma_{M,k}\}_{M\in\mathbb{N}}
uniformly converges to a curve

\gamma_{k}\in\mathrm{AC}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
Indeed,

(C.8)

\begin{split}\int_{0}^{T}\psi\Big(\frac{1}{N}|\dot{\gamma}_{M,k}(t)|\Big)\,{\mathrm{d}}t&\leq\int_{0}^{T}\psi\Big(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}|\dot{\gamma}_{M,i}(t)|\Big)\,{\mathrm{d}}t\\
&=\sum_{n=1}^{2^{M}}\tau_{M}\,\psi\Big(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\left|x^{n}_{M,\sigma^{0,n}_{M}(i)}-x^{n-1}_{M,\sigma^{0,n-1}_{M}(i)}\right|}{\tau_{M}}\Big)\\
&=\sum_{n=1}^{2^{M}}\tau_{M}\,\psi\Big(\frac{1}{\tau_{M}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}|x-y|\,{\mathrm{d}}\varrho_{M}^{n-1,n}(x,y)\Big)\\
&=\sum_{n=1}^{2^{M}}\tau_{M}\,\psi\Big(\frac{1}{\tau_{M}}W_{1}(\mu_{M}^{n-1},\mu_{M}^{n})\Big)\\
&\leq\sum_{n=1}^{2^{M}}\tau_{M}\,\psi\Big(\frac{1}{\tau_{M}}\int_{(n-1)\tau_{M}}^{n\tau_{M}}|\mu^{\prime}|(t)\,{\mathrm{d}}t\Big)\\
&\leq\sum_{n=1}^{2^{M}}\int_{(n-1)\tau_{M}}^{n\tau_{M}}\psi(|\mu^{\prime}|(t))\,{\mathrm{d}}t=\int_{0}^{T}\psi(|\mu^{\prime}|(t))\,{\mathrm{d}}t,\end{split}

where we employed the definition of the optimal plan in (
C.7
),
(
C.5
), Jensen’s inequality, and (
C.6
).

Since
\gamma_{M,k}(0)=x_{k}(0)
for any
M\in\mathbb{N}
, and (
C.8
) and (
C.6
) hold, by Ascoli-Arzelá Theorem the sequence
\{\gamma_{M,k}\}_{M\in\mathbb{N}}
is compact in
C([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Furthermore, by (
C.8
) and the lower semicontinuity of the functional

\gamma\mapsto\int_{0}^{T}\psi\left(\frac{1}{N}|\dot{\gamma}(t)|\right){\mathrm{d}}t

w.r.t. weak convergence in
\mathrm{AC}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we get
\gamma_{k}\in\mathrm{AC}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
Moreover, if
t=n_{0}\tau_{M_{0}}
for some
M_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
and
n_{0}\in\{0,1,\ldots,2^{M_{0}}\}
, then

\gamma_{M,k}(t)
is constant for any
M\in\mathbb{N}
,
M>M_{0}
and the claim is proved.

Defining

(C.9)

\eta_{M}:=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\delta_{\gamma_{M,i}},\qquad\eta:=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\delta_{\gamma_{i}},

from the convergence of
\gamma_{M,i}
to
\gamma_{i}
it follows that

\eta_{M}
weakly converges to
\eta
as
M\to+\infty
.
Moreover, if
t=n_{0}\tau_{M_{0}}
for some
M_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
and
n_{0}\in\{0,1,\ldots,2^{M_{0}}\}
, then

(e_{t})_{\sharp}\eta_{M}=\mu_{t}
for any
M\in\mathbb{N}
,
M>M_{0}
.
Then, by the continuity of
t\mapsto\mu_{t}
and of
t\mapsto(e_{t})_{\sharp}\eta
,
we conclude that
(e_{t})_{\sharp}\eta=\mu_{t}
and
\mu_{t}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\delta_{\gamma_{i}(t)}

for all
t\in[0,T]
.

It remains to define a vector field
v
such that (
C.4
) and
(
C.2
) hold, also showing that
v
is uniquely characterized by (
C.2
).

Since
\gamma_{i}\in\mathrm{AC}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{d})
for any
i=1,\ldots,N
,
the Borel set
A:=\{t\in[0,T]:\exists\,k\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\text{ such that }\gamma_{k}\text{ is not differentiable at }t\}

is
{\mathcal{L}}_{T}
-negligible. Moreover, the sets

(C.10)

N_{i,k}:=\{t\in[0,T]\setminus A:\gamma_{i}(t)=\gamma_{k}(t),\dot{\gamma}_{i}(t)\neq\dot{\gamma}_{k}(t)\}

satisfy
{\mathcal{L}}_{T}(N_{i,k})=0
for any
i,k\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
.
We define
\tilde{N}:=\Big(\bigcup_{i,k\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}N_{i,k}\Big)\bigcup A

noticing that
{\mathcal{L}}_{T}(\tilde{N})=0
so that
{\mathcal{L}}_{T}\otimes\mu_{t}(\tilde{N}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})=0
,
and

S:=\{(t,\gamma_{i}(t)):t\in[0,T],\ i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\}=\mathop{\rm supp}\nolimits(\mu_{t}\otimes{\mathcal{L}}_{T})
.
We can thus define a Borel vector field
v:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
by

v(t,x):=\begin{cases}0&\text{if }t\in\tilde{N}\text{ or }(t,x)\in\big([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\big)\setminus S,\\
\dot{\gamma}_{i}(t)&\text{if }x=\gamma_{i}(t)\ \text{for $t\in[0,T]\setminus\tilde{N}$ and some }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\end{cases}

so that
\dot{\gamma}_{i}(t)=v_{t}(\gamma_{i}(t))
for every
t\in[0,T]\setminus\tilde{N}
.
It is then easy to check that (
C.1
) and (
C.2
) hold.

Let us eventually check that (
C.2
) uniquely characterizes
v(t_{0},x_{0})

for every
(t_{0},x_{0})\in S\setminus(\tilde{N}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
Notice that for every
\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
we have

(C.11)

\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varphi\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{t}\Big|_{t=t_{0}}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\nabla\varphi(\gamma_{i}(t_{0}))\cdot\dot{\gamma}_{i}(t_{0})=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\nabla\varphi(\gamma_{i}(t_{0}))\cdot v({t_{0}},\gamma_{i}(t_{0})).

Setting
K:=\{k\in\{1,\ldots,N\}:\gamma_{k}(t_{0})\neq x_{0}\}
and
r_{0}:=\min\{|\gamma_{k}(t_{0})-x_{0}|:k\in K\}>0
, for every
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
we
can find
a test function

\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that
\mathop{\rm supp}\nolimits\varphi\subset B_{r_{0}}(x_{0})

and
\nabla\varphi(x_{0})=\xi
:
(
C.11
) then yields

(C.12)

\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varphi\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{t}\Big|_{t=t_{0}}=\frac{n}{N}\xi\cdot v({t_{0}},x_{0})\quad\text{where}\quad n:=N-\#K.

Since
\xi
is arbitrary, (
C.12
) uniquely characterizes
v(t_{0},x_{0})
in terms of
\mu
.
∎

Appendix D
Finite Partitions

In this section we provide a proof of Proposition
4.16
.
For sake of clarity, we divide the statement of Proposition
4.16
in three separate lemmas of independent interest.

Given a standard Borel space
(\Omega,\mathfrak{B},\mathbb{P})
, in Lemma
D.2
we construct a family of algebras
\mathfrak{B}^{n}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, satisfying the finite approximation property of Definition
4.15
.
Then we fix
(\Omega,\mathfrak{B},\mathbb{P})=([0,1],{\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{L}}_{1})
, where
{\mathcal{B}}
is the Borel
\sigma
-algebra and
{\mathcal{L}}_{1}
the Lebesgue measure restricted to the interval
[0,1]
.
With this choice of parametrization space, in Lemma
D.3
we show that the family of algebras
{\mathcal{B}}^{N}
associated to the uniform partition of
[0,1]
with elements’ size
1/N
satisfies the finite approximation property.
Finally, we combine the previous results in Lemma
D.4
, where we consider a general standard Borel space
(\Omega,\mathfrak{B},\mathbb{P})
and
\mathbb{P}
is without atoms.
This is possible thanks to the following fundamental result on Borel equivalence of Probability spaces
(see e.g.
[
50
, Chapter 15, Theorem 9]
).

Proposition D.1
.

Let
\Omega
be a Polish space and
\mathbb{P}\in\mathscr{P}(\Omega)
without atoms.
Then there exist a Borel set
\Omega_{0}\subset\Omega
such that
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{0})=0
, a Borel set
I_{0}\subset[0,1]
such that

{\mathcal{L}}_{1}(I_{0})=0
and a bijective function
\psi:\Omega\setminus\Omega_{0}\to[0,1]\setminus I_{0}
such that

\psi
and
\psi^{-1}
are Borel,
\psi_{\sharp}\mathbb{P}={\mathcal{L}}_{1}
and
(\psi^{-1})_{\sharp}{\mathcal{L}}_{1}=\mathbb{P}
.

The first part of Proposition
4.16
is restated in the following Lemma.

Lemma D.2

(Proposition
4.16
, part 1)
.

Let
(\Omega,\mathfrak{B},\mathbb{P})
be a standard Borel space.
Then there exists a family of finite algebras

\mathfrak{B}^{n}\subset\mathfrak{B}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, satisfying the finite approximation property of Definition
4.15
.

Proof.

Since
(\Omega,\mathfrak{B},\mathbb{P})
is standard Borel we can choose a Polish topology
\tau
such that
\mathfrak{B}={\mathcal{B}}_{(\Omega,\tau)}
, then there exists a countable basis
{\mathcal{A}}=\{B^{i}:i\in\mathbb{N}\}
of its topology. Then

{\mathcal{B}}_{(\Omega,\tau)}=\sigma(\{B^{i}:i\in\mathbb{N}\})
.
We define
\mathfrak{B}^{1}:=\sigma(B^{1})
and
\mathfrak{B}^{n}:=\sigma(\{B^{n}\}\cup\mathfrak{B}^{n-1})
.
It follows from the definition that
\mathfrak{B}^{n}\subset\mathfrak{B}^{n+1}
for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and

{\mathcal{B}}_{(\Omega,\tau)}=\sigma\left(\bigcup_{n=1}^{+\infty}\mathfrak{B}^{n}\right)
.

For any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the finite algebra
\mathfrak{B}^{n}
induces a
minimal (with respect to the inclusion) partition of
\Omega
, denoted by

{\mathcal{P}}^{n}=\{A_{k}^{n}:k=1,\ldots,k(n)\}\subset\mathfrak{B}^{n}
.
Then for any
A_{k}^{n+1}\in{\mathcal{P}}^{n+1}
there exists

h\in\mathbb{N}
such that
A_{h}^{n}\in{\mathcal{P}}^{n}
and
A_{k}^{n+1}\subset A_{h}^{n}
.

We define the sequence of linear operators
P_{n}:L^{1}(\Omega;E)\to L^{1}(\Omega;E)
defined by

P_{n}g:=\sum_{k=1}^{k(n)}\mathds{1}_{A^{n}_{k}}\,\fint_{A^{n}_{k}}g(\omega)\,{\mathrm{d}}\mathbb{P}(\omega),

with the convention that
\fint_{A^{n}_{k}}g(\omega)\,{\mathrm{d}}\mathbb{P}(\omega)=0
if
\mathbb{P}(A^{n}_{k})=0
.

It is simple to prove that

(D.1)

\|P_{n}g\|_{L_{\mathbb{P}}^{1}(\Omega;E)}\leq\|g\|_{L_{\mathbb{P}}^{1}(\Omega;E)},\qquad\forall\,g\in{L_{\mathbb{P}}^{1}(\Omega;E)}.

Given a Borel function
g:\Omega\to E
such that
g\in L_{\mathbb{P}}^{1}(\Omega;E)
, we define
g^{n}:=P_{n}g
and we prove that
the properties of Definition
4.15
hold.

Property
(i)
is obvious since
g^{n}
is constant on the elements of the partition
{\mathcal{P}}^{n}\subset\mathfrak{B}^{n}
.

Property
(ii)
follows from the fact that (see for instance
[
26
, Corollary 8, p. 48]
)

\fint_{A}g(\omega)\,{\mathrm{d}}\mathbb{P}(\omega)\in\mathrm{\overline{co}}\left(g(A)\right),\quad\forall\,A\in{\mathcal{B}}_{(\Omega,\tau)}:\mathbb{P}(A)>0.

In order to prove property
(iii)
we start with the particular case
g=a\mathds{1}_{A}

for a given Borel set
A
and a given
a\in E
.
Since
(\Omega,\tau)
is Polish, for any
\varepsilon>0
there exists an open set
A_{\varepsilon}
and a compact set
K_{\varepsilon}
such that

K_{\varepsilon}\subset A\subset A_{\varepsilon}
and
\mathbb{P}(A_{\varepsilon}\setminus K_{\varepsilon})<\varepsilon
.
Since
A_{\varepsilon}
is union of elements of the basis
{\mathcal{A}}
, there exists a finite covering of
K_{\varepsilon}
of the form

\{B^{j}:j\in J\}\subset{\mathcal{A}}
, for a suitable finite
J\subset\mathbb{N}
, such that

\cup_{j\in J}B^{j}\subset A_{\varepsilon}
.
Since

P_{n}g-g=a\sum_{k=1}^{k(n)}\frac{\mathbb{P}(A_{k}^{n}\cap A)}{\mathbb{P}(A_{k}^{n})}\mathds{1}_{A_{k}^{n}}-a\mathds{1}_{A},

by setting
n_{\varepsilon}:=\max J
, it holds that

\|P_{n}g-g\|_{L_{\mathbb{P}}^{1}(\Omega;E)}\leq\|a\mathds{1}_{A_{\varepsilon}}-a\mathds{1}_{A}\|_{L_{\mathbb{P}}^{1}(\Omega;E)}\leq\|a\|_{E}\ \mathbb{P}(A_{\varepsilon}\setminus A)<\|a\|_{E}\,\varepsilon\ ,\quad\forall\ n\geq n_{\varepsilon}\ .

Since
P_{n}
is linear, then
(iii)
holds for any
g
simple function.
In the general case, take
g\in L_{\mathbb{P}}^{1}(\Omega;E)
and
\varepsilon>0
, and let
g_{\varepsilon}:\Omega\to E
be a simple function such that

\|g-g_{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\mathbb{P}}^{1}(\Omega;E)}<\varepsilon
.
Observing that

\|P_{n}g-g\|_{L_{\mathbb{P}}^{1}(\Omega;E)}\leq\|P_{n}g-P_{n}g_{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\mathbb{P}}^{1}(\Omega;E)}+\|P_{n}g_{\varepsilon}-g_{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\mathbb{P}}^{1}(\Omega;E)}+\|g_{\varepsilon}-g\|_{L_{\mathbb{P}}^{1}(\Omega;E)},

by (
D.1
) and property
(iii)
applied to
g_{\varepsilon}
it holds that

\limsup_{n\to+\infty}\|P_{n}g-g\|_{L_{\mathbb{P}}^{1}(\Omega;E)}\leq 2\varepsilon
and we conclude.

Finally, property
(iv)
follows from the measurability of
G
, Fubini Theorem and the definition of
P_{n}
.
∎

Consider now the Polish space
([0,1],{\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{L}}_{1})
.

Lemma D.3

(Proposition
4.16
, part 2)
.

For any
N\in\mathbb{N}
we define
I_{k}^{N}:=[(k-1)/N,k/N)
,
k=1,\ldots,N-1
,
I_{N}^{N}:=[(N-1)/N,1]
.
If
E
is a Banach space,
g\in L^{1}([0,1];E)
and

g^{N}:=\sum_{k=1}^{N}\mathds{1}_{I^{N}_{k}}\,\fint_{I^{N}_{k}}g(s)\,{\mathrm{d}}s,

then

(D.2)

\lim_{N\to+\infty}\|g^{N}-g\|_{L^{1}([0,1];E)}=0.

Moreover, the family of finite algebras
{\mathcal{B}}^{N}:=\sigma(\{I^{N}_{k}:k=1,\ldots,N\})
,
N\in\mathbb{N}
, satisfies the finite
approximation property of Definition
4.15
.

Finally, if
g\in L^{p}([0,1];E)
, for some
p\in(1,+\infty)
, then
\lim_{N\to+\infty}\|g^{N}-g\|_{L^{p}([0,1];E)}=0
.

Proof.

For any
x\in[0,1]
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists a unique
k(x,N)
such that
x\in I_{k(x,N)}^{N}
.
From the definition of
I^{N}_{k}
it follows that
I_{k(x,N)}^{N}\subset B_{1/N}(x)
.
Since

g^{N}(x)-g(x)=\sum_{k=1}^{N}\mathds{1}_{I^{N}_{k}}(x)\,\fint_{I^{N}_{k}}(g(s)-g(x))\,{\mathrm{d}}s,

then

\|g^{N}(x)-g(x)\|_{E}\leq 2\fint_{B_{1/N}(x)}\|g(s)-g(x)\|_{E}\,{\mathrm{d}}s.

By the Bochner version of the Lebesgue differentiation Theorem (see for instance
[
26
, Theorem 9, p. 49]
)
we obtain that

\lim_{N\to+\infty}\|g^{N}(x)-g(x)\|_{E}=0
for
{\mathcal{L}}_{1}
-a.e.
x\in[0,1]
.

Since
\int_{0}^{1}\|g(x)\|_{E}\,{\mathrm{d}}x<+\infty
there exists a convex, increasing, superlinear function
\psi:[0,+\infty)\to[0,+\infty)

such that
\int_{0}^{1}\psi\left(\|g(x)\|_{E}\right)\,{\mathrm{d}}x<+\infty
.
Since

\|g^{N}(x)\|_{E}\leq 2\fint_{B_{1/N}(x)}\|g(s)\|_{E}\,{\mathrm{d}}s,

by Jensen’s inequality,

\begin{split}\int_{0}^{1}\psi\left(\|g^{N}(x)\|_{E}\right)\,{\mathrm{d}}x&\leq 2\int_{0}^{1}\psi\left(\fint_{B_{1/N}(x)}\|g(s)\|_{E}\,{\mathrm{d}}s\right)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\\
&\leq 2\int_{0}^{1}\fint_{B_{1/N}(x)}\psi\left(\|g(s)\|_{E}\right)\,{\mathrm{d}}s\,{\mathrm{d}}x\\
&=N\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\mathds{1}_{(x-1/N,x+1/N)}(s)\psi\left(\|g(s)\|_{E}\right)\,{\mathrm{d}}s\,{\mathrm{d}}x\\
&=N\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\mathds{1}_{(s-1/N,s+1/N)}(x)\psi\left(\|g(s)\|_{E}\right)\,{\mathrm{d}}s\,{\mathrm{d}}x\\
&\leq 2\int_{0}^{1}\psi\left(\|g(s)\|_{E}\right)\,{\mathrm{d}}s<+\infty,\end{split}

which implies the equi-integrability of the sequence
\|g^{N}\|_{E}
.
Then (
D.2
) holds.

The finite approximation property for
{\mathcal{B}}^{N}
follows as in the proof of Lemma
D.2
. The final assertion is a consequence of the equi-integrability of the sequence
\|g^{N}\|^{p}_{E}
.
∎

Lemma D.4

(Proposition
4.16
, part 3)
.

Let
(\Omega,\mathfrak{B},\mathbb{P})
be a standard Borel space and
\mathbb{P}
without atoms. Then there exists a family
\mathfrak{B}^{N}\subset\mathfrak{B}
,
N\in\mathbb{N}
, satisfying the finite approximation property of Definition
4.15
such that the associated minimal partition
{\mathcal{P}}^{N}=\{A_{k}^{N}:k=1,\ldots,N\}
contains exactly
N
elements and
\mathbb{P}(A^{N}_{k})=\frac{1}{N}
, for
k=1,\ldots,N
.

Proof.

Let
\tau
be a Polish topology on
\Omega
such that
\mathfrak{B}={\mathcal{B}}_{(\Omega,\tau)}
.
Let also
\Omega_{0}
,
I_{0}
,
\psi
,
\psi^{-1}
be given by Proposition
D.1
.
Using the notation of Lemma
D.3
we define the sets

A^{N}_{1}:=\psi^{-1}(I^{N}_{1}\setminus I_{0})\cup\Omega_{0}
and

A^{N}_{k}:=\psi^{-1}(I^{N}_{k}\setminus I_{0})
for
k=2,\ldots,N
.

It is immediate to prove that
\mathbb{P}(A^{N}_{j})=\frac{1}{N}
for
j=1,\ldots,N
and
\{A^{N}_{1},\ldots,A^{N}_{N}\}
is a partition of
\Omega
.
Moreover, given a Banach space
E
and
g\in L^{1}_{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega;E)
, we denote by

\tilde{g}:=g\circ\psi^{-1}\in L^{1}([0,1];E)
.
Denoting by
\tilde{g}^{N}
the sequence given by Lemma
D.3
applied to
\tilde{g}
,
we define
g^{N}:=\tilde{g}^{N}\circ\psi
and the finite approximation property for
(\Omega,{\mathcal{B}}_{(\Omega,\tau)},\mathbb{P})
follows by
Lemma
D.3
.

∎
